| 2026-08-26 | Eval judge tolerates a non-JSON response -- `ai/src/canopica_ai/policy_intelligence/eval/judge_model.py`, `ai/tests/test_judge_model.py` (+5 tests, 134 -> 139) | Run `32935635062`'s `ai-eval` died after answering **all 8 questions**, ~19 minutes in, on `ValueError: Evaluation LLM outputted an invalid JSON. Please use a better evaluation model.` -- DeepEval's `trimAndLoadJson` rejecting one `contextual_precision` judge response out of roughly a dozen. Not the faithfulness regression this job has been carrying, and not infrastructure: a **third shape of "the upstream provider returned garbage"**, after the 200-with-`error`-body (2026-08-24) and the real non-2xx (2026-08-25) this same retry gate already handles. It is the one that looks least like a failure -- HTTP 200, no `error` key, a `content` string present, simply not JSON despite `response_format: json_schema` with `strict: true` having been sent. Handled where the other two already are, in `generate()`'s existing attempt loop, rather than bolted on: a schema'd call whose body will not load is retried like any other transient upstream fault. Retrying is not merely analogous to the 404 case but justified the same way -- OpenRouter routes `:free` requests to whichever provider endpoint is free at that instant, so the next attempt is a genuinely different roll rather than a deterministic repeat, which is also why `temperature: 0` does not make it pointless. Exhausting the attempts raises carrying **the offending body verbatim**, since whether the provider returned prose, a refusal, or truncated JSON is the entire diagnosis and is unrecoverable from a CI log otherwise. The JSON check deliberately **mirrors DeepEval's own leniency** (first `{` to last `}`, then `json.loads`) instead of validating strictly against the schema: being stricter than the consumer would reject responses DeepEval would have accepted and turn a working run into a retry storm -- a fenced ```json block is explicitly tested as accepted, not retried, and a schemaless call is tested to still accept plain prose. Applied tonight rather than held, at the user's explicit direction, as its own commit so both tonight's commits push together and cost one CI iteration instead of two. Verified by the same full-suite run as the row above: **`make lint` clean, `make test` exit 0, AI suite 134 -> 139.** Not yet exercised against the real judge -- that happens on the next CI run. |
| 2026-08-26 | CI caching, cleanup correctness, and the GitHub-hosted/self-hosted switch -- `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `infra/docker-compose.yml`, `infra/azure/ci-runner/{cloud-init.yaml,ci-step-timings.sh}` | **The prune hook was about to delete the cache the disk was grown to hold, and it took measuring to see it.** The user asked whether pruning should spare the cached images and Ollama models. Checking the VM rather than reasoning about it: **16 tagged images totalling 20.7GB and zero dangling ones**, every one of the 16 a base image CI re-pulls each run (ollama 8.43GB, three Airflow variants at 3.74GB, opensearch 2.55GB, metabase 1.81GB, keycloak/grafana/prometheus/postgres). The disk-pressure branch's `docker image prune -af` would have removed all of them -- discarding exactly what the 32GB->64GB resize had been paid for the day before. What makes this easy to get wrong is that `docker system df` calls 5.96GB of it "reclaimable": that counts any image with no *currently running* container, which on an idle runner is all of them. Policy now splits by cost-to-recreate rather than by Docker's own word -- containers/networks/volumes every job, build cache *bounded* every job, tagged images never on a schedule, and `-af` demoted to a **staged** emergency at a 6GB floor (down from 12GB): drop build cache, re-measure, touch images only if that was not enough, and say so loudly. **Three separate silent-no-op bugs were found by testing the hook against the live daemon instead of reading it.** (1) `docker volume prune -f` removes only *anonymous* volumes; every volume Compose creates is named, so the line reported success and skipped all of them. That is not cosmetic -- it is the direct cause of this run's `e2e-ai` failure, `duplicate key value violates unique constraint "worker_email_key"`, which is this file's own known Keycloak-subject-reissue collision (see Open questions) promoted from a hand-reachable dev annoyance to a structural every-run failure, because the operational Postgres volume now outlives the Keycloak container that reseeds against it. Fixed with `-af`. (2) The build-cache bound was first written `--keep-storage`, which Docker 29.7.2 has **removed**, not deprecated -- behind the hook's deliberate `|| true` it would have done nothing forever. (3) With the correct `--max-used-space`, the budget turns out to apply to the *reclaimable* cache, not the total: at 5.803GB total / 1.373GB reclaimable a 4GB budget correctly pruned nothing, while a 1GB budget reclaimed 483MB. The comment now describes that semantic rather than the one assumed. **Verified live on the VM, not by inspection**: running the new `job-started-hook.sh` took local volumes **2 -> 0** while leaving **16/16 tagged images and all 2.2GB of Ollama models untouched** -- the exact before/after the policy claims. **Ollama's models moved off `actions/cache` onto the VM's own disk** (`OLLAMA_MODELS_DIR=/opt/ollama-models`, read by `infra/docker-compose.yml`'s bind mount and defaulting to the in-workspace path a GitHub-hosted runner still needs), and pre-warmed there (2.2GB) so the next run measures steady state. It must sit outside the workspace because `actions/checkout` runs `git clean -ffdx` on every job. **The wall-clock case for this is smaller than it first looked and the cost case is bigger** -- worth stating precisely, because the convenient number was wrong: `actions/cache` cost 389s in run `32927971610` but only **55s** (2 x ~27s) in `32935635062`, the 362s outlier being a cold-cache *save*, not the steady state. The durable saving is the data: ~2.3GB restored twice per run is **~4.6GB through the NAT Gateway at $0.045/GB (Azure retail API) = ~$0.21/run, against ~$0.11/run of compute** -- i.e. the cache was costing roughly twice what the VM it fed did. The cache step, its paired `chmod`, and the preinstalled-toolchain `rm -rf` (whose paths do not exist on the VM) are now gated on `runner.environment == 'github-hosted'`, the `rm -rf` branched *inside* its step so it still overlaps the pull where that matters. **The runner switch is done, and is two repo variables rather than the one originally sketched.** `CI_LIGHT_RUNNER` (8 cheap jobs) and `CI_HEAVY_RUNNER` (3 expensive ones) each hold a JSON runner spec, **unset meaning `ubuntu-latest`** -- the fallback deliberately being the path that needs no infrastructure to exist, so a lapsed subscription or destroyed VM is survived by clearing variables rather than by editing eleven `runs-on:` lines under pressure. Two, not one, because the planned end state needs them to differ: from ~Sep 2026 the cheap jobs want GitHub-hosted *parallelism* back while the heavy ones stay on the VM. `start-runner`/`stop-runner` gate their **steps**, never the jobs -- all eleven jobs carry `needs: [start-runner]` and a skipped job skips its dependents, so gating the jobs would silently disable the entire workflow the moment the variables were cleared. Both variables were set to `["self-hosted","canopica-heavy"]` before commit, because **without that the next push would have relocated all of CI to `ubuntu-latest` silently.** `ci-step-timings.sh` added so every number above can be re-taken rather than trusted, reporting per *step* because a job that got faster by losing a step and one that got faster on a quieter box are indistinguishable at job level. Totals for the record: **2687s** (`32927971610`) and **2933s** (`32935635062`). **Result: `make lint` clean; `make test` green end to end (exit 0 -- Java, UI, data-platform 29, AI 139).** **Committed and deliberately NOT pushed**, at the user's request to review the diff first. **One thing this leaves out of band and easy to lose**: `cloud-init.yaml` runs only on a VM's *first* boot, so the live VM's hooks and `.env` were regenerated from the committed file and applied in place (Terraform would otherwise replace the VM and destroy the registered runner) -- the two are in sync as of this commit, and any future edit to that file needs the same manual step. |
| 2026-08-26 | Runner capacity + end-of-job cleanup + eval progress output -- `infra/azure/ci-runner/{main.tf,cloud-init.yaml}`, `ai/src/canopica_ai/policy_intelligence/eval/run_eval.py`, `ai/tests/test_eval_gate.py` (+2 tests, 132 -> 134) | **Disk raised 32GB -> 64GB, from a measurement rather than a hunch**: after the 20.2GB emergency prune left 20GB free, *two* e2e jobs took it back to **6.9GB** -- roughly 13-17GB of images cycled per run (three ~3.65GB Airflow variants plus api/ui/opensearch/ollama). At 32GB the new hook's disk-pressure branch would therefore have fired on essentially every job, which *works* but discards the Docker layer cache every time and rebuilds from scratch -- the exact wall-clock cost the threshold exists to avoid. +$2.40/month (E4 -> E6 StandardSSD) buys the cache back and makes the threshold an exception again rather than the steady state. Resized live while the VM was already deallocated post-cancel (no extra downtime); Ubuntu's own boot-time growpart extended the filesystem with no manual step -- confirmed `61G total / 39G free / 38% used`, and the explicit `growpart` attempted alongside it failed harmlessly only because the device is `nvme1n1`, not `sda`. `main.tf` updated in lockstep. **Added `ACTIONS_RUNNER_HOOK_JOB_COMPLETED`, at the user's prompting** -- they asked directly whether cleanup also runs when CI stops, and it did not. The start hook stays the *guarantee* (it runs however the previous job ended, including cancelled/killed, which no end hook does); the completed hook fixes *when* space is reclaimed, since with start-only cleanup a heavy job's containers and images sit on disk until the next job begins -- and if it was the run's last job, straight through deallocation into the next run hours later. That is precisely how run `32932666191` went 20GB -> 6.9GB. Same prune set minus the disk-pressure branch (reclaiming here is never urgent, so it keeps the layer cache). Verified live: it removed 3 containers the cancelled `ai-eval` left behind. **`run_eval` now reports progress**, also user-prompted: this job is the slowest in CI (13-35 min) and emitted *nothing* between start and final scores, so a working run and a hung one produced byte-identical logs -- the only way to tell them apart was opening a shell on the runner and reading CPU usage, which happened twice tonight and is not a diagnostic anyone should need. One `eval progress: N/total questions` line per question plus a closing awaiting-judges line, on **stderr** so it never contaminates the `metric: value` stdout lines read against `baseline.json`. Two tests pin it (position/total for every question; stderr-not-stdout), because an unasserted signal is one that silently stops working. **Run `32932666191` cancelled deliberately, not abandoned**: it already carried 2 failures, so its verdict was fixed and `ai-eval`'s remaining ~30 minutes could not change it -- while the thing actually needing verification (`e2e-data-platform`/`e2e-ai` under the disk fix) had never once run green. Re-running was strictly more informative than finishing. **Verified**: `make lint` clean; `make test` green across every layer -- Java 107, web 20 (6 files), data-platform 29 passed/9 deselected, `ai/` **134** passed/22 deselected (up 2). **Not yet verified**: no CI run has yet gone green end to end under the corrected hooks and the larger disk; that is the next push's job. |
| 2026-08-26 | Fix (incomplete-fix correction): the pre-job cleanup hook pruned containers only, and the disk filled instead -- `infra/azure/ci-runner/cloud-init.yaml` | **The memory half of the fix is confirmed working**: in run `32932666191`, jobs started with **~6.9GB available** against 657MB before, and the hook logged `removing 15 container(s) left by a previous job` when tested live. **The same run then failed two jobs on `No space left on device`.** Root filesystem at **100%, 184MB free**, holding 20.7GB of images of which **16.94GB was unreferenced**, plus 2.94GB of build cache and 896MB of dead volumes; a one-off `docker image prune -af` + `builder prune -af` reclaimed **20.2GB** and took the disk to 35%. **Why the first fix was wrong, stated plainly**: it scoped itself to containers on the reasoning that the proven failure was *memory*, and that images and volumes cost disk rather than RAM. That reasoning was true and beside the point -- "GitHub-hosted runners gave every job a clean machine" is a claim about **all** Docker state, and on a long-lived VM the disk fills for exactly the same structural reason the memory did. Naming the other three forms as deliberately out-of-scope is what made an incomplete fix read as a complete one. This is the second time in this repo a fix has been aimed at one true-but-partial cause (cf. the 2026-08-25 concurrency/docs-filter row). **Corrected hook**: containers, networks and volumes prune unconditionally; **images and build cache prune on disk pressure** (`< 12GB` free) rather than every job, because a full prune forces every image to rebuild next job and that is real wall-clock -- keep the layer cache while there is room, discard it only when finishing the job matters more than finishing it fast. Verified live: the hook ran, took the has-room branch correctly at 20GB free, and logged both disk and memory. **Third-order failure worth recording, since it cost the most confusion**: when the disk hit 100%, the runner could not write its *own* diagnostic logs (`##[error]No space left on device : '/opt/actions-runner/_diag/pages/...'`), which orphaned its GitHub session -- the service then sat in a reconnect loop (`Connected to GitHub` / `A session for this runner already exists`, repeating) while GitHub still reported it `status=online busy=false` and the next job sat `queued` against a session that no longer existed. Indistinguishable from the SNAT idle-timeout symptom from the outside, and **not** that: the NAT Gateway was already in place and egress verified. `systemctl restart` on the runner service cleared it and the queued job dispatched in one second. The generalizable point: a disk-full condition on a self-hosted runner does not present as a disk error, it presents as a runner that looks healthy and silently stops working. **Not yet verified**: the disk-pressure branch of the hook has not yet been exercised by a real run crossing the 12GB floor. |
| 2026-08-26 | Docs: production-tradeoffs doc gains §4.18 (orchestration in commercial vs. Gov cloud) and §4.19 (what actually forces Azure Government, and what doesn't) -- `docs/design/2026-08-21-tech-stack-and-production-tradeoffs.md` | **Prompted by a question rather than by a code change**, and researched against primary Microsoft sources rather than answered from memory -- which mattered, because the first answer given verbally was partly wrong and the research corrected it. **Finding 1 (corrects a common assumption, including one stated in this session before checking)**: IRS Publication 1075 is *not* the forcing function for Azure Government that it is widely believed to be. Microsoft's own IRS 1075 compliance offering lists applicability as **both** Azure and Azure Government, notes both hold a FedRAMP High P-ATO from the JAB on which the IRS 1075 control scope rests, provides contractual amendments for both, and states outright that "the need to meet your IRS 1075 compliance requirements isn't a deciding factor for choosing your cloud environment." The real Gov-cloud differentiators are *contractual and personnel* -- US-only data storage and access limited to screened US persons -- which bear most on export-control regimes (EAR/ITAR/DoE 10 CFR 810), a different concern from FTI handling. **Finding 2 (directly affects this repo's own stated reference architecture)**: **Azure Container Apps is not available in Azure Government** -- Microsoft's Azure Government service-comparison page lists only Service Fabric and Container Registry under Containers, with no announced date for Container Apps -- while **AKS is GA across US Gov Virginia/Arizona/Texas**. So the "containers, not clusters" reasoning that makes Container Apps the right call in commercial Azure inverts in Gov cloud: the simpler option is the one that does not exist, and AKS becomes the default *by elimination* rather than by outgrowing a PaaS. The Platform-tier Runtime and Cloud rows were corrected in place accordingly, since as written they implied a portability the service catalog does not support. **Finding 3 (on-point for this domain specifically)**: IRC 6103(l)(7) bars human services agencies from contracting for services that disclose FTI to contractors -- which reads like it forecloses cloud hosting -- but the IRS Office of Safeguards' cloud guidance treats FIPS 140-validated encryption with the agency retaining **sole ownership of the keys** as a *logical barrier* that permits it. That makes customer-managed keys in an HSM the load-bearing control for this workload, not the cloud or the orchestrator; §4.15's PII-tokenization caveat is this repo's deliberately-weaker local stand-in for it. **No code changed**; this is a documentation-accuracy fix plus new analysis, verified against `learn.microsoft.com` primary sources (the IRS 1075 offering page and the Azure Government service-comparison page) rather than secondary summaries. |
| 2026-08-26 | Fix: a regression this session introduced -- `api/src/test/resources/docker-java.properties` pinned an API version the CI runner supports and a developer laptop does not | **Caught by the full-suite-before-push rule, not by CI**: `make test` failed **100 of 107 Java tests**, every one a Testcontainers-backed test, all tracing to `com.github.dockerjava.api.exception.BadRequestException: Status 400: {"message":"client version 1.51 is too new. Maximum supported API version is 1.47"}`. **Cause**: the `api.version=1.51` fix committed earlier the same night (`f44d4e7`) was chosen by reading only the CI runner's daemon (Docker 29.7.2, range 1.40-1.55) and verifying live *there* -- correct for that box, and wrong everywhere else. This is a classpath resource, so it applies to every daemon that runs this suite, and Docker Desktop 27.4.0 on the dev laptop caps at API 1.47 (`docker version` reports `Server API: 1.47 | MinAPI: 1.24`). The property written up as that fix's *virtue* -- that it travels with the code rather than living on one runner -- is exactly the property that made getting the number wrong break everyone. **Fix**: pin inside the *intersection* of every daemon that runs the suite rather than inside any single one -- CI `1.40-1.55` and laptop `1.24-1.47` intersect at **`1.40-1.47`**; `1.44` sits inside with margin at both ends (clear of Docker 29's 1.40 floor, well under 1.47, and satisfied by any engine from Docker 25.0 onward). Nothing Testcontainers does here needs a recent API, so the lowest workable value is the safest rather than a compromise; the file now states the intersection explicitly and tells the next reader to re-derive the floor before raising it. **Verified**: `./mvnw -B verify` **107 tests, 0 failures, 0 errors, BUILD SUCCESS** on the same laptop that had just failed 100; then full `make test` green across every layer -- Java 107, web 20 (6 files), data-platform 29 passed/9 deselected, `ai/` 132 passed/22 deselected. `make lint` clean (2 pre-existing `only-export-components` warnings, untouched). **The transferable lesson, since this is the second time this exact fix has been wrong**: "verified live" is only as good as the set of environments it was verified *on*. One box passing is evidence about one box. |
| 2026-08-26 | Fix: the structural cause behind three CI failures in run `32927971610` -- containers surviving between jobs on the long-lived runner -- plus the SNAT idle-timeout bug from the row below, now permanently fixed rather than worked around. `infra/azure/ci-runner/{cloud-init.yaml,main.tf}`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml` | **Root cause, proven from the run's own compose output rather than inferred**: `ci.yml` was written against GitHub-hosted `ubuntu-latest`, where every job gets a brand-new VM, so nothing ever needed cleaning up. On this one long-lived VM nothing tears containers down between jobs, and `docker compose up` reuses whatever is already there. The logs show the accumulation directly: `e2e-ai` started with `infra-ollama-1 Running` + `infra-opensearch-1 Running` left from `ai-eval`, then `e2e-data-platform` started with `jaeger`/`api`/`keycloak`/`postgres` still `Running` on top of *those* -- roughly 13 containers on a 7.8GB box with **zero swap** (`Mem: 7.1Gi used / 657Mi available` measured at the end of `ai-eval`). Both failures are that one cause wearing different clothes: `container infra-ollama-1 is unhealthy` (its 5s healthcheck timed out 12 times) and Airflow's `No response from gunicorn master within 120 seconds`. This is why it kept presenting as a *new* bug each time -- whichever container loses the memory race is the one that fails, so the symptom moves while the cause does not. **Fix, and why this shape**: a pre-job cleanup hook registered via the runner's own `ACTIONS_RUNNER_HOOK_JOB_STARTED`, removing leftover containers and networks before every job. Deliberately a runner hook rather than a step in each job, so it covers all 12 current jobs *and every job added later* with nothing to remember -- the same "you have to remember it" gap `canopica-task-checkpoint` step 5 exists to close. Deliberately at job *start*, not job end, because an end-hook never runs when a job is cancelled or the runner drops mid-run (both have happened here). Volumes are deliberately left alone: the proven failure is memory, volumes cost disk not RAM, and dropping them would add re-indexing time to every job while fixing nothing shown to be broken. 4GB of swap added alongside -- explicitly *not* the fix, just headroom on a box half the size of the 16GB runner `ci.yml` was written against, turning a marginal failure into a slow pass. **Verified live on the VM, not assumed**: the hook was run for real and reported `removing 15 container(s) left by a previous job`, leaving `container count = 0` -- 15 stale containers from run `32927971610` were still resident, which is the root cause confirmed directly rather than argued. **SNAT idle-timeout bug (previous row's Gap 1) now actually fixed**: with the user's explicit go-ahead once it was established this subscription is an Azure free trial (`quotaId: FreeTrial_2014-09-01`, spending limit On) holding $200 of credit that expires ~30 days from signup and cannot be spent at this project's ~$0.11-per-run burn rate, the drafted NAT Gateway was applied -- targeted (`-target` on the 4 NAT resources only) specifically because a full apply wanted to **replace the VM**, since `cloud-init.yaml` feeds `user_data` and Azure cannot change that in place; replacing it would have destroyed the registered runner and every cached image and model. Verified: egress IP from the VM is `20.29.74.242`, exactly matching `azurerm_public_ip.nat`, with `idle_timeout_in_minutes = 30` against Azure's fixed ~4. The VM keeps its no-public-IP property (NAT Gateway is outbound-only). **Dated commitment, not open-ended**: this NAT Gateway + its Standard public IP bill ~$36/month *continuously* with no deallocate-between-runs, and are only affordable inside the trial window. The decision on record is to **destroy them before the trial converts to pay-as-you-go** (`terraform destroy -target=azurerm_subnet_nat_gateway_association.this -target=azurerm_nat_gateway_public_ip_association.this -target=azurerm_nat_gateway.this -target=azurerm_public_ip.nat`), after which the SNAT bug returns and the workaround is again a manual runner-service restart. Confirm the exact expiry in the portal (Subscriptions -> this subscription); the trial started on or before 2026-08-25, when the VM was created. **cloud-init.yaml updated in lockstep** with both changes so a future rebuild starts from this state rather than regressing, matching the pattern the `unzip`/Docker-API rows below already established -- with the known consequence that `terraform plan` will show the VM as needing replacement until it is next rebuilt. **Deliberately NOT covered by this fix**: `ai-eval`'s failure in the same run (`faithfulness = 0.625` vs baseline `0.750`, `citation_grounded_rate 0.500`, but `contextual_precision`/`contextual_recall` both `1.000`) is a *generation*-quality regression with retrieval provably intact -- the eval gate doing its job, a different class of problem, and there is no evidence memory pressure caused it. Recorded here as open rather than folded into an infra fix it has not been shown to belong to. **Not yet verified**: the next CI push is the real test of the cleanup hook under actual job-to-job load. |
| 2026-08-26 | Fix: two more real gaps found on the runner's second and third real attempts -- a dead outbound connection that silently stopped job dispatch, and the same Docker-client bug from the row below turning out to need a genuinely different fix than first applied -- `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `api/src/test/resources/docker-java.properties` (new), `infra/azure/ci-runner/cloud-init.yaml` | **Gap 1 (recurring, not yet permanently fixed)**: run `32924144504` queued 8 jobs against an online, idle (`busy: false`) runner that dispatched none of them for 8+ minutes -- `ss` on the VM showed the runner's one connection to `broker.actions.githubusercontent.com` still `ESTAB` locally with zero Recv-Q/Send-Q, and its own diagnostic log went completely silent after "Listening for Jobs," no error, no reconnect attempt. Root cause: this subnet has no NAT Gateway, only Azure's default outbound SNAT, which silently drops idle TCP flows after a fixed ~4-minute timeout with no FIN/RST to either side -- the runner's long-poll connection looked alive forever but was actually dead. A manual `systemctl restart` on the runner service immediately unstuck the backlog, confirming the diagnosis. **A NAT Gateway fix (configurable idle timeout) is drafted in `infra/azure/ci-runner/main.tf` but deliberately not applied or committed** -- raised with the user as a real cost tradeoff (~$36/month, always billing, vs. ~$3.65/month for a direct public IP that would regress the "no public IP on the VM" security property) and the user chose to hold off rather than have either applied unilaterally. This recurrence risk is accepted, not resolved, until that decision is made. **Gap 2**: the previous row's `DOCKER_API_VERSION=1.51` `.env` fix did not work -- confirmed live on a second CI run hitting the identical `UnixSocketClientProviderStrategy` failure. Tested directly against the real failing test on the VM (not re-guessed): both `DOCKER_API_VERSION` and `DOCKER_HOST` env vars were tried explicitly, and the failure log showed *every* strategy docker-java attempted -- including `EnvironmentAndSystemPropertyClientProviderStrategy`, the one meant to read exactly these vars -- failing with the identical hardcoded-1.32 error, proving the broken handshake happens before docker-java ever consults them. A classpath `docker-java.properties` resource (`api.version=1.51`) is docker-java's own documented, higher-precedence config mechanism; tested the same way (live, against the real `SchemaMigrationTest`, via Testcontainers) and confirmed working -- `Tests run: 3, Failures: 0, Errors: 0`, container started, Flyway migrated, Spring context up. Lands as `api/src/test/resources/docker-java.properties` (travels with the code, so it also helps anyone who clones this repo and runs its tests locally against a similarly-new Docker Engine, not just this one runner); the ineffective `.env`-based attempt removed from `cloud-init.yaml` rather than left as a second, dead mechanism. **Gap 3 (smaller, same run)**: `hashicorp/setup-terraform@v4`'s default `terraform_wrapper: true` needs Node.js, which this runner doesn't have and nothing in the `terraform` job's steps actually needs (no step captures wrapped output) -- set `terraform_wrapper: false` instead of adding a Node.js dependency for a feature unused here. **Not yet verified**: the next CI push is the real test of the docker-java.properties and terraform_wrapper fixes together; Gap 1's recurrence risk stays open pending the NAT Gateway/public-IP decision. |
| 2026-08-25 | Fix: `azure/login` failing `AADSTS700213` (no matching federated identity) on every CI attempt after the GitHub billing block/cloud-init.yaml`, live-applied to the running VM | **Trigger**: once the OIDC fix above landed, run `32923099481` reached the runner for real and surfaced two genuine gaps between this minimal cloud-init VM and a GitHub-hosted `ubuntu-latest` image, neither previously exercised since nothing had run on `canopica-heavy` before this run. `terraform` job: `hashicorp/setup-terraform@v4` failed outright, "Unable to locate executable file: unzip" -- GitHub-hosted images bundle `unzip`; this Ubuntu 24.04 cloud image never did. `java` job: every Testcontainers-backed test failed with a cascading `NoClassDefFoundError` on `AbstractPostgresTest`; the real root, several frames down, was `UnixSocketClientProviderStrategy: failed with exception BadRequestException (Status 400: client version 1.32 is too old. Minimum supported API version is 1.40)` -- confirmed via `docker version` on the VM (Engine 29.7.2, installed fresh via `get.docker.com`, minimum supported API 1.40) that Testcontainers-Java's initial Docker-client detection probe hardcodes API 1.32 for its handshake, a known upstream testcontainers-java/docker-java gap against Docker Engine releases this recent -- confirmed against public GitHub issues (`testcontainers/testcontainers-java#11232`, `#11419`, `#11240`) documenting the same class of break on Docker 24.x-29.x, not guessed from the error text alone. **Fix**: `unzip`/`zip` added to cloud-init's package list; `DOCKER_API_VERSION=1.51` written to `/opt/actions-runner/.env` -- a real, documented GitHub Actions self-hosted-runner feature (every job the runner executes inherits it) and a real supported docker-java config knob (not a workaround-hack) that skips the broken auto-detection entirely. 1.51 chosen with headroom inside the daemon's actual supported range (1.40-1.55), not pinned to either edge. Both applied live to the already-running VM via `az vm run-command` (packages installed, `.env` written, runner service restarted) so the next push doesn't need a VM rebuild to pick them up; `cloud-init.yaml` updated in lockstep so a future `terraform destroy`/`apply` rebuild starts from the same fixed state rather than regressing. `terraform fmt -check`/`terraform validate` clean. **Not yet verified**: the next CI push is the real test of both fixes together -- this row records the fix and its reasoning, not a confirmed-green run. |
| 2026-08-25 | Fix: `azure/login` failing `AADSTS700213` (no matching federated identity) on every CI attempt after the GitHub billing block cleared -- `infra/azure/ci-runner/{main,variables}.tf` | **Trigger**: once the user raised the Actions spending limit, `changes` finally ran, but `start-runner` failed `azure/login@v2` immediately with `AADSTS700213: No matching federated identity record found for presented assertion subject 'repo:bsreecharanreddy@283869510/canopica@1341374123:ref:refs/heads/main'` (run `32914410256`) -- a numeric-ID-qualified subject neither federated credential's `subject` (plain `repo:bsreecharanreddy/canopica:ref:refs/heads/main`) could ever match. **Root-caused live, not guessed**: GitHub's changelog confirms any repository renamed on or after 2026-07-15 automatically gets the newer immutable-subject format (`repo:<owner>@<owner_id>/<repo>@<repo_id>:ref:...`), and this repo was renamed the same day this Terraform root was first applied -- so the plain-name subject configured at apply time was already stale before its first real use. The owner/repo IDs in the error were independently verified against `gh api repos/bsreecharanreddy/canopica` (not just trusted from the error text) and matched exactly. **Fix**: two new variables (`github_owner_id`, `github_repo_id`) and both federated credentials' `subject` rebuilt in the immutable format; `terraform plan` showed exactly the expected 2-resource in-place update (0 add, 0 destroy), applied clean. **Verified**: re-triggered run in progress at time of writing -- this row records the fix and its reasoning, not yet a confirmed-green run; see the next row (if a further fix was needed) or `docs/demo.md`-style confirmation once one lands. |
| 2026-08-25 | Self-hosted CI runner widened to every job except `changes`/`start-runner`/`stop-runner` -- `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, design doc addendum (`2026-08-25-self-hosted-ci-runners.md` §6), `infra/azure/ci-runner/README.md` | **Trigger**: the very first push after provisioning the runner (commit range `f232a4c..e4bda91`) failed CI in 5 seconds flat, on every job including the trivial ones (run `32913936148`) -- "recent account payments have failed or your spending limit needs to be increased." This repo's GitHub Actions minutes (2000/month cap) were fully exhausted for real, and unlike graceful degradation this blocks every `ubuntu-latest` job outright, including `changes` itself. **User-diagnosed correctly, not root-caused independently by inference**: the user identified the minutes exhaustion as the cause before any log was pulled. **Fix**: every job now runs on `canopica-heavy` except `changes`/`start-runner`/`stop-runner`, which are structurally irreducible -- something has to decide to start the VM and then start it, and that can't run on the VM being started. `start-runner` also dropped its `code`-flag gate (now unconditional, since even the 8 previously-cheap jobs need the VM on every push) and its `needs: [changes]` wait, so it starts in parallel with change-detection rather than after it. **Two real consequences, stated rather than hidden**: this cuts GitHub-hosted minutes by roughly 90%+ per run but does not eliminate them -- the 3 residual control jobs still need *some* working GitHub billing to run at all, so the account-level block above still needs clearing on GitHub's own settings page regardless of this change. And a single self-hosted runner processes one job at a time, so jobs that used to run in GitHub-hosted parallel (up to 11 of them) now serialize on one VM -- real-code-push wall-clock time goes up, accepted as the right trade given the alternative is CI that cannot start at all. YAML parse-checked; no application code changed. **Not yet verified**: no successful CI run under this wiring yet -- blocked on the user clearing the GitHub billing block, tracked as the next real test, not asserted here. |

A full `az vm list-skus --location centralus --all` dump (not another single-SKU guess) showed this is a subscription-level VM-family restriction on this new/free-trial subscription, not a per-region capacity issue as first assumed -- `az vm list-usage` had shown free quota the whole time, which is what pointed at "restriction," not "capacity," as the real category. `Standard_D2s_v7` (same 2 vCPU/8GB shape) came back with `restrictions: []` and 4/4 free vCPU quota under `Standard Dsv7 Family`; `variables.tf`'s `vm_size` default and this directory's `README.md` were updated to match, with the real finding recorded in place of the original capacity-guess. **Verified live end to end, not just applied**: `terraform apply` created all 4 resources (subnet, NIC, NSG association, VM) clean; cloud-init reported `status: done` (Docker + runner binary installed); the runner registered and confirmed **online** on GitHub (`gh api .../actions/runners` shows `canopica-ci-runner-vm`, label `canopica-heavy`, `status: online`); the three OIDC repo variables (`AZURE_CLIENT_ID`, `AZURE_TENANT_ID`, `AZURE_SUBSCRIPTION_ID`) are set from `terraform output`; the VM was deallocated afterward (`az vm get-instance-view` confirms `VM deallocated`) so it isn't billing compute at rest. `ci.yml` then got the design doc §5 wiring in the same pass: a new `start-runner` job (OIDC `azure/login`, `az vm start`, gated on `needs.changes.outputs.code == 'true'` -- necessary and sufficient, since `e2e-data-platform`'s own condition is exactly that flag and the other two heavy jobs' conditions AND with it) precedes the 3 heavy jobs (`e2e-data-platform`, `e2e-ai`, `ai-eval`), whose `runs-on` moved to `[self-hosted, canopica-heavy]`; a `stop-runner` job (`if: always() && needs.start-runner.result == 'success'` -- the second clause specifically so a docs-only push, where `start-runner` never ran, doesn't try to deallocate a VM nothing here started) follows them. `terraform fmt -check`/`terraform validate` clean on the new root; YAML parse-checked. **Not yet verified**: no real CI run has exercised this wiring end to end (`start-runner` actually reaching the VM inside Actions, the runner actually picking up a queued job) -- that first live run is the real verification and is still open. Before this repo goes public: `canopica-heavy` must be decommissioned or fork-restricted per the design doc's own §5 caveat -- not yet done, tracked there, not duplicated here. |
| 2026-08-25 | Fix: `analytics_copilot`'s real-model tool-calling variance, root-caused rather than left as the known issue two rows below -- `ai/src/canopica_ai/analytics_copilot/{tools,service}.py`, `ai/tests/test_analytics_copilot.py` (+9 tests, 24 -> 33) | **Trigger**: CI run `32854476102` (the IES->Canopica rename push) failed `End-to-end -- AI layer` on `MetricCompilationError` -- the model called `query_metric(metric_names=['determinations'], group_by=['determination__is_expedited'])`, the *count* metric grouped by a dimension only `avg_processing_days`'s semantic model has. Per this skill's own rule (attribute before fixing), the single observed shape was not trusted as the whole problem: an 8-live-call local batch against the identical real question (real Keycloak token, real `llama3.2:3b`, real MetricFlow compile) surfaced two more distinct, previously-unnamed failure modes -- a filter *verbatim-repeating* its own group_by dimension (`filters=["determination__is_expedited"]`), which compiles (a bare boolean column is a valid `WHERE` clause) and silently drops every False row instead of failing loudly; and, dominant in a follow-up 6-of-6 batch, the same mistake in comparison form (`filters=["determination__is_expedited = true"]`) -- an equality filter on the exact dimension being grouped by can only ever leave one group standing. **Three real fixes, not one**: (1) `service.ask()` gained one bounded corrective retry -- on a `MetricCompilationError` or a `pydantic.ValidationError`, the compiler's/validator's own rejection (which already names the valid options) is fed back to the model for exactly one more try; wrong twice still fails the caller, never silently, and never "falls back to something else" on its own (Task 5 plan Step 5's original guarantee, preserved). (2) `QueryMetricArgs` gained `RedundantGroupByFilterError`: a filter targeting the same dimension as any group_by entry, bare or with a real comparison, is rejected before MetricFlow ever sees it -- closing the exact silent-wrong-answer path found above; a filter on a genuinely *different* dimension stays allowed (regression-tested). (3) the tool description's `metric_name` catalog had been one string per metric ("name (description)"), which the model sometimes echoed back whole as the argument value -- split into a bare name list and a separate descriptions sentence. **Two self-inflicted regressions caught in the same investigation and fixed before landing, not shipped**: an early revision of the retry-prompt's own example filter (`"determination__program_code = 'SNAP'"`) got copied back verbatim regardless of the actual question in 6 of 6 live calls -- removed the example entirely, replaced with a rule ("filters is OPTIONAL, usually omit it"). Then the corrective-retry prompt itself rendered the previous call via Python's `!r` (single-quoted repr, not JSON) -- 5 of 5 retries in the next batch echoed that same single-quoted style back for group_by, failing validation a second time for a reason unrelated to what was actually being corrected. Fixed at the source (`json.dumps`, never `repr`) and defensively (the existing JSON-encoded-string coercion, from the 2026-08-24 fix below, widened to also accept a Python-literal-style string via `ast.literal_eval` -- narrowly, list-only, same tolerance shape as the JSON case). **Measured live, before and after, not asserted**: matched 10-live-call batches against the real stack went 6-wrong/10 (silently or loudly) before the retry-prompt fix to 9-correct/10 after it; the one residual failure across three 10-sample batches is a `MetricCompilationError` after two consecutive different wrong-metric guesses -- a loud, honest failure every time, never once a silently wrong answer. Whether that residual ~10% single-run tail risk warrants a further, deliberate mitigation (e.g. a bounded pytest-level rerun specifically for this live-model test class) was raised with the user as an open question rather than decided unilaterally -- `pytest-rerunfailures` is only present as `deepeval`'s own transitive dependency, not a pattern this project has adopted anywhere else. **Unrelated local-environment breakage found and fixed along the way, blocking verification rather than the CI failure itself**: both `ai/` and `data-platform/`'s `.venv`s had shebangs baked in from before the `integrated-eligibility-system` -> `canopica` directory rename and needed rebuilding; the local Keycloak container's dev-mode database had, for the same reason, never re-imported past its pre-rename `ies-workers`/`ies-citizens` realms (`IGNORE_EXISTING` import strategy) and needed recreating -- not `make down`, which would also have dropped Postgres's operational data; Keycloak's dev-mode state lives in that one container's own writable layer with no named volume, so recreating only it was sufficient and left every other service's data untouched. `make test`/`make lint` green across the monorepo (Java 107, web 20, data-platform 29, `ai/` 154 [132 non-e2e + 22 e2e, up from 145]). |

So `ai-eval` now has its own `ai_eval` filter output, a deny-list of every top-level directory its steps provably never read from (`portal/`, `rules-engine/`, `identity/`, `data-platform/`, plus the existing docs/`.claude/`/`infra/azure/` set), same fail-open philosophy as the existing `code` filter (unrecognized path defaults to relevant, so the failure mode stays "ran unnecessarily," never "silently skipped a run that should have caught something"). What this run *does* prove is that the new gating doesn't break `ai-eval` on a push that should still trigger it. Also added to the skill: a local-repro loop (`make up && make e2e`, plus `ai-eval`'s own three-step body run directly against a local stack) to catch a wrong fix before it costs another push's worth of quota -- the same shape of fix as this skill's own rule 6, applied to Actions minutes instead of debugging time. | 2026-08-25 | Fix: the eval judge treated a transient OpenRouter 404 as fatal and discarded its own diagnosis; Policy Q&A's three abstention causes were indistinguishable -- `ai/src/canopica_ai/policy_intelligence/eval/judge_model.py`, `ai/src/canopica_ai/policy_intelligence/qa/service.py`, `ai/tests/test_judge_model.py`, `ai/tests/test_abstention_reason.py` (new) | **The circuit-breaker fix in the row below is confirmed working, which is the headline**: run `32811993194`'s `ai-eval` completed *all eight questions'* retrieval and generation -- **24m39s** of real work -- with zero breaker failures, against 11m48s and 5s in the two runs before it. Three live probes settled it: the pinned model is present in OpenRouter's public model list; a plain chat call returns 200; and a call carrying the exact `json_schema` structured-output shape DeepEval sends also returns 200. A model that does not exist can do none of those, so the 404 is OpenRouter's `:free`-variant capacity behaviour, which is transient. **This therefore reverses a decision made earlier the same day** -- 404 had been added to the non-retryable set as "the model does not exist", the textbook reading, pinned by `test_a_real_404_status_is_not_retried`. That test is rewritten rather than deleted, and the guard it was really protecting is kept: a 401 still fails on the first attempt (`test_a_client_configuration_error_is_still_not_retried`). The reversal rests on an asymmetric payoff, not on a preference: a genuinely missing model now fails on the first call *and says why*, while a transient one otherwise throws away 25 minutes of finished work. The same run's `e2e-ai` failed separately and, notably, **not** on the `analytics_copilot` variance flagged in the row below -- that test passed this time, confirming it intermittent -- but on `TestAnswerDenial` getting `abstained=True`. **Also unattributable, and for the same shape of reason**: `_abstain()` had three genuinely different callers (weak retrieval, prompt too long, ungrounded after one retry) all producing a byte-identical `abstained=True, citations=[]`. Added `AbstentionReason` (a `StrEnum` on `QaAnswer`, in-memory only -- no schema migration, and it lands in the model's own repr where a failing assertion actually prints it). Stated plainly because it matters: **that makes the next occurrence attributable, not less likely** -- the underlying live-model variance is not fixed, and fixing it needs the attribution first. TDD throughout, RED confirmed for the right reason on both (the 404 propagated unretried; the raised message was literally the uninformative `HTTP 404 Not Found` that had made CI opaque). Full suite green: Java 107 (BUILD SUCCESS), Web 20 across 6 files, data-platform 29, **ai 123 (116 -> 123)**; `make lint` clean across all four languages. |
| 2026-08-25 | CI fix: `cancel-in-progress` killed in-flight **main** runs, so a commit could land merged and unverified -- `.github/workflows/ci.yml` only | **A previous fix in this log was wrong, and this corrects it rather than adding to it.** Run `32806255794` was recorded above as a docs-only push cancelling a run that was mid-way through proving a real fix green; the fix applied then was adding `.claude/` to the docs filter. That was aimed at the wrong layer: the filter governs which *jobs* do work, but `concurrency` is evaluated at **workflow** level before any job is considered, so the previous run is cancelled whether or not the new run's jobs would all skip. Filtering jobs could never fix a cancellation that happens above the jobs. Proven by it happening a second time, identically: the docs-only skill commit `6586f48` cancelled run `32811724784`, which was minutes from returning the first real verdict on the circuit-breaker fix in the row below. Now `cancel-in-progress: ${{ github.ref != 'refs/heads/main' }}` -- superseding is still free on a PR branch, where only the latest commit's verdict matters, but on main every commit is permanent history with a per-commit result recorded in this very log, so a cancelled run means a merged commit nobody verified. Note this is a correction of a *diagnosis*, not just of a config value: the first round treated "the docs commit ran the expensive suite" (real, and really fixed) as the whole harm, when the harm that actually mattered was the cancellation, which that fix did not touch. Exactly the failure the new `canopica-recurring-ci-failure` skill's first rule exists to prevent -- naming the mechanism before fixing it. |
| 2026-08-25 | Fix: `hybrid_search` treated ml-commons' memory circuit breaker (HTTP 429) as a fatal error rather than as backpressure -- `ai/src/canopica_ai/policy_intelligence/retrieval.py`, `ai/tests/test_retrieval_backpressure.py` (new, +3 tests) | **The recurring "Memory Circuit Breaker is open" failure is finally attributed from evidence rather than inference, and the diagnostics ported in the row below paid for themselves on their first red run** (`32810042677`). First, the previous row's reorder fix is confirmed working: `index` ("indexed 46 chunks") and `search_pipeline` ("reranker model_id=lJJDN6ABK61AjGusS2Qn") both went green, where the prior run died five seconds into the *first* `hybrid_search`; this run got through **11m48s of real retrieval** before failing. What the new diagnostics then showed, at the moment of failure: **every OpenSearch core breaker reported `tripped: 0`** (request, fielddata, in_flight_requests, and parent at 383.6mb of a 1.8gb limit), which rules out OpenSearch's own breakers entirely and leaves ml-commons'. Of ml-commons' three, **the message itself names which**: the disk breaker says "Disk Circuit Breaker" and the host-RAM one says "Native Memory Circuit Breaker", so plain "Memory Circuit Breaker" is the JVM-heap breaker -- `plugins.ml_commons.jvm_heap_memory_threshold`, confirmed in force at the `92` that `search_pipeline.py` had already raised it to from `85`. **And it is transient, not a live-set problem**: the JVM logged a collection three seconds after the failure (`[gc][410] overhead, spent [391ms] collecting in the last [1.5s]`, 04:58:19 against a 04:58:16 failure) and the heap read **18%** one second after that. So the heap sawtoothed past 92%, ml-commons sampled `heapUsedPercent` at the peak, and a ~400ms collection reclaimed nearly all of it -- the garbage was never pressure. **Raising the threshold a third time was therefore rejected as a non-fix**: `85 -> 92` had already failed at exactly this, and a sawtooth's peak approaches 100% by construction, so tuning only moves the dice. A 429 is by definition a "retry shortly" signal and one GC is all "shortly" needs to mean, so `hybrid_search` now retries on the 429 *status* (deliberately not on the message string: OpenSearch returns 429 for both `circuit_breaking_exception` and a full-thread-pool `es_rejected_execution_exception`, the same signal wanting the same handling), 4 attempts with linear 2s/4s/6s backoff -- the same shape and constant as `judge_model.py`'s OpenRouter retry. TDD, RED confirmed for the right reason first: the breaker error propagated to the caller and `_MAX_SEARCH_ATTEMPTS` did not exist. **Retry is explicitly not suppression** -- `test_a_breaker_that_never_clears_still_raises` pins that a cluster genuinely out of heap still fails the gate, and `test_a_non_backpressure_error_is_not_retried` pins that a 404 propagates on the first attempt rather than hiding a real bug behind seconds of pointless backoff. The retry count lands on the existing retrieval span, so a run that only succeeded after backing off stays distinguishable from one that never contended. Full suite green: Java 107 (BUILD SUCCESS), Web 20 across 6 files, data-platform 29, **ai 116 (113 -> 116)**; `make lint` clean across all four languages. **Not fixed here, and not the same failure**: the same run's `e2e-ai` job failed separately on the known `analytics_copilot` live-model variance (`{True: 6.5}` vs the expected `{True: 6.5, False: 25.0}`) -- `llama3.2:3b` picked the correct metric *and* the correct dimension, then added a filter nobody asked for, dropping one group. That is a judgment call about whether to weaken a live-model assertion, deliberately left open rather than bundled into a commit about the circuit breaker. |
| 2026-08-25 | CI fix: `ai-eval` provisioned the corpus in the opposite order to the fixture it imitates, had none of the circuit-breaker diagnostics, and a nested `.claude/**/*.md` counted as code -- `.github/workflows/ci.yml` only | Run `32808001972` went 11 of 12 green (`End-to-end -- AI layer` **passed**, clearing the analytics_copilot variance flake) with `ai-eval` failing on `TransportError(429, 'circuit_breaking_exception', 'Memory Circuit Breaker is open')` -- thrown from the *first* `hybrid_search`, five seconds into `run_eval.py`. **Two things that same log proves, before anything else**: `CANOPICA_OTEL_ENABLED: false` is in the job env and not one OTLP retry line appears (the previous run had dozens), so the tracer fix above held; and execution died at retrieval, long before any judge call, so the separately-confirmed OpenRouter free-tier daily cap (since lifted -- account now reads `is_free_tier: false`, and a live probe of the pinned judge model returns a clean 200 at `cost: 0`, still a `:free` model) was not the blocker either. **Host memory ruled out by direct A/B evidence from this one run, not by inference**: both jobs ran the same commit on the same runner type, and `e2e-ai` -- the one that *passed* -- did so under strictly worse pressure, six containers at 7.1Gi/7.8Gi host memory with 865Mi of swap in use (opensearch 2.453GiB, ollama 2.913GiB, keycloak 452MB, portal-api 364MB, postgres 28MB, jaeger 22MB), while `ai-eval` failed with two containers and far more headroom. That is the second independent confirmation of the row below's finding that this breaker is not about host RAM. **The real differentiator, found by diffing the two jobs' setup**: `ai/tests/conftest.py`'s `indexed_corpus` fixture indexes *first* and provisions the search pipeline *second*; this job did the reverse, so the ~90MB ONNX reranker was deployed at 04:20:44-04:20:59 and then sat resident in OpenSearch's 2g heap for the entire 46-second indexing burst (04:20:59-04:21:45) before the first query, instead of being loaded after indexing finished. Reordered to match the sequence actually observed green; verified `index.py` contains no reference to the search pipeline, so running it first is safe. **The diagnostics gap is the more durable finding, and is the same meta-pattern as this session's other two**: the "Diagnose OpenSearch's circuit breakers on failure" step and the `df -h`/`free -h`/`docker stats` steps were added to `e2e-ai` when that was the only job running OpenSearch; `ai-eval` was created later (Task 7) with the same OpenSearch + reranker + corpus-index workload and inherited none of them -- so when the same breaker tripped here, the failure was once again unattributable between ml-commons' three byte-for-byte identically-worded breakers (JVM heap, host memory, free disk), which is precisely the two-day ambiguity those steps exist to end. Ported verbatim (confirmed byte-identical by comparing the parsed YAML `run` blocks, not by eye), plus a `df -h /` baseline on green runs. **Third fix, self-inflicted and caught the same night**: the docs deny-list `^(docs/|[^/]*\.md$)` only ever matches *top-level* markdown, so `.claude/skills/canopica-task-checkpoint/SKILL.md` counted as code -- a pure-docs commit queued the full ~20min e2e suite and, via `cancel-in-progress`, killed run `32806255794` while it was mid-way through proving the previous commit green (that run is `cancelled`, not `failed`). Added `\.claude/` to the deny-list: it holds only local developer tooling -- skills, and a commit-time hook CI never executes -- so nothing under it can change a job's result. Regex extracted back out of the committed YAML rather than tested from memory, and checked across 8 cases with real system grep: docs-only/skill-only/hook-only all `code=false`, while mixed skill+code, code-only, nested non-docs `.md`, a `ci.yml` change, and an unknown diff base all correctly stay `code=true` -- real code can never skip e2e. (A first pass reported the mixed case as `code=false`, which would have been a serious bug; that turned out to be this dev host's `grep` being a `ugrep` wrapper, not a flaw in the regex -- re-run with `command grep` to match CI's GNU grep.) **Stated plainly, for the third time in this log on this specific breaker: one green run will not prove the reordering fix.** The diagnostics are behavior-neutral (they only add observability on failure), so if the next run is green the reorder is the credible cause, and if it trips again the breaker finally names itself instead of costing another round of inference. Task 7 and Task 8 both remain implemented and unit/lint-verified but **not yet CI-confirmed end to end**. |

| 2026-08-25 | Phase 2 Task 8 (AI observability) -- `ai/src/canopica_ai/common/observability.py` (new), `ai/tests/test_ai_observability.py` (new), `ai/src/canopica_ai/policy_intelligence/retrieval.py`, `ai/src/canopica_ai/common/llm_client.py`, `ai/src/canopica_ai/policy_intelligence/qa/service.py`, `ai/src/canopica_ai/analytics_copilot/service.py`, `ai/src/canopica_ai/policy_intelligence/rule_authoring/service.py`, `ai/src/canopica_ai/dashboard_assist/service.py`, `ai/src/canopica_ai/config.py` (+`otel_exporter_endpoint`) | All green: Java 119 (12 rules + 107 portal), web 20, data-platform 29, `ai/` 131 (111 non-e2e + 20 e2e, up from 121). **Live-verified against real Jaeger, not just asserted**: a real question through the real service, against the real corpus and the real local model, produces a `retrieval cfr-part-273` span and a `policy_qa.answer_general` span carrying a populated `gen_ai.usage.input_tokens` and a `rag_citation_grounded` attribute, all queryable from Jaeger's own API (`TestAgainstRealJaeger`, e2e). **Design doc §2.8's own caveat -- verify the spec live at implementation time, don't trust the plan's prose -- caught real drift**, checked via WebFetch/WebSearch against the current spec rather than assumed: the GenAI semantic conventions left the main `semantic-conventions` repository for their own (`open-telemetry/semantic-conventions-genai`, v1.42.0, 2026-06-12); the finish-reason attribute is `gen_ai.response.finish_reasons` (plural, array-typed -- the plan's prose named the singular form); and `gen_ai.provider.name` is a required discriminator whose enum has no value for Ollama or any self-hosted model, so `"ollama"` is used as a deliberate off-enum value, documented inline rather than left looking like a typo. Every `gen_ai.*` attribute remains Development-stability, none Stable, as the design doc's own caveat anticipated. **Two real corrections against the plan's own prose, both found while implementing rather than assumed from it**: (1) the plan said to wrap each capability's own `service.py` LLM call site, but `LlmResponse` carries only `text` -- token counts don't exist until Ollama's raw response is parsed, so instrumentation lives in the one place that can see it, `common/llm_client.py`'s `OllamaClient._post`/`generate_tool_call`, rather than requiring `LlmResponse` (and every test double of it) to grow fields purely to ferry telemetry through four separate call sites; each capability's `service.py` still gets its own `traced_ai_operation` *capability* span (`policy_qa.answer_general`, `analytics_copilot.ask`, `rule_authoring.propose_parameter_changes`, `dashboard_assist.propose_dashboard`), just not the per-call `gen_ai.*` attributes themselves. (2) The plan listed `infra/docker-compose.yml` as a file to modify (pointing `ai`-side services at Jaeger); no change was needed, because `ai/` has no Compose service of its own -- every entry point runs via `uv run` on the host, the same pattern `data-platform` already established, so the existing `otel_exporter_endpoint` default of `localhost:4318` already reaches Jaeger's published port without any wiring. `top_k` on a retrieval span is recorded under this project's own `canopica.retrieval.top_k` namespace rather than the spec's `gen_ai.request.top_k`, which means the *sampler's* top-k -- a real, different thing -- documented and regression-tested (`test_top_k_is_recorded_under_this_project_s_own_namespace`) rather than left as a quiet semantic collision. **A real bug in the test itself, caught and fixed before trusting the result**: the e2e test's first two attempts locally produced false failures from the *test harness*, not production code -- confirmed by directly querying Jaeger's stored traces rather than guessing. First, a crashed early run (against an unready OpenSearch, before the corpus's rerank ML model had deployed) left a stale, incomplete trace under the newly-introduced `canopica-ai` service name; polling for "any trace exists" matched that stale trace instead of waiting for a fresh one, so `_find_trace_spans` now takes a `since_micros` cutoff (Jaeger's own `start` query param) excluding anything exported before the test's own call. Second, the capability span closes and exports strictly *after* its retrieval/generation children (it's the outermost `with` block), so polling for "any data" could return once Jaeger had indexed the children but not yet the parent; `_find_trace_spans` now takes an `until` predicate and polls until the caller's actual required evidence is present, not until anything is. README's architecture diagram checked -- no change: Jaeger/OTel was never drawn as a diagram node even for Phase 1b's own original observability work (prose-only in the README), and Task 8 adds no new component and no new depicted data flow. Also flipped Task 3 (rule-authoring copilot)'s checkboxes in the plan file to `[x]` -- stale since that task's own commit (`ae1e1df`), pure docs drift caught while touching this same file, not new work. |
| 2026-08-25 | Fix: the OpenRouter judge's retry logic only ever handled OpenRouter's 200-wrapped-error shape, never a real HTTP error status -- `ai/src/canopica_ai/policy_intelligence/eval/judge_model.py`, `ai/tests/test_judge_model.py` | Task 7's third CI confirmation run (`32800860332`) failed `ai-eval` on a genuinely new, third failure shape: `httpx.HTTPStatusError: Client error '404 Not Found'` raised from `judge_model.py:121`'s own `response.raise_for_status()`, checked from the real job log rather than assumed. Root cause, found by reading the code rather than guessing: `raise_for_status()` ran unconditionally *before* the retry-eligibility check below it, so any real non-2xx HTTP status -- 404, but just as much a genuine 429/502/503 -- raised immediately and skipped the retry loop entirely. The existing retry tests (`TestTransientUpstreamOverloadIsRetried`, added for the prior 502) only ever faked OpenRouter's *other* error shape (a 200 response with an `error` key in the body), so they could not have caught this; live-tested the model, endpoint, and exact schema-constrained request shape directly against OpenRouter with `curl` immediately after the failure and got a clean 200, confirming the 404 was a real, transient edge-level rejection rather than the model being gone or the request being malformed. Fixed with TDD (`TestRealHttpStatusErrorsAreRetriedToo`, 2 tests written first and watched fail with the exact reproduced traceback): a real HTTP status is now checked against the same `_RETRYABLE_ERROR_CODES` the 200-wrapped case already used, before `raise_for_status()` ever runs, and a non-retryable real status now raises the same `RuntimeError` shape as the other failure path (previously it would have let the raw `httpx.HTTPStatusError` escape uncaught, a second, smaller inconsistency fixed in the same pass). `make lint` clean; `ai/` 121 total (102 non-e2e + 19 e2e, up from 119 -- +2 non-e2e for the two new tests). |

Root cause: the 240s `ollama_timeout_seconds` was measured (2026-08-23) against this repo's then-longest real prompt (~12,186 characters); `golden_set.yaml`'s own questions assemble prompts up to ~15,522 characters (several large retrieved CFR chunks together), and CPU-bound prompt evaluation cost scales with token count, so the margin that held for other callers wasn't enough here. Raised to 480s with the real evidence recorded inline, a shared setting so every `OllamaClient` caller gets the same margin rather than special-casing the eval gate. `ai-eval`'s own `timeout-minutes` raised 30 -> 35 to keep real margin at the new per-call ceiling across up to 8 questions each potentially needing a grounding retry. The run's *other* failure, `End-to-end -- AI layer`'s `TestAskWithARealModel::test_a_real_question_resolves_to_the_correct_metric_and_number` (`assert {True: 6.5} == {True: 6.5, False: 25.0}`), is the same known analytics_copilot LLM tool-calling variance already recorded two rows down (a third distinct failure shape on the same real-model test this session: JSON-encoded-string args, wrong metric selection, and now an unwanted filter) -- unrelated to anything Task 7 touched, left as-is rather than chased. **Both predictions confirmed on the next run** (`32797053521`): `End-to-end -- AI layer` passed clean, and no `ReadTimeout` recurred -- the timeout fix held. `ai-eval` failed again, but on a genuinely *different* and real bug, checked from the log rather than assumed: `psycopg.OperationalError: connection refused` on port 5432. `answer_general()` records every answer's provenance to `ai.policy_qa_answer` (design doc §2.2), which needs a live, migrated Postgres -- and the `ai-eval` job only brings up `opensearch`+`ollama`. The first run never reached that write because the timeout crashed first, so the gap was real from the moment the job was written and simply masked. **Fixed at the right layer rather than by growing the job**: standing up `portal-api`+Postgres+Flyway just to satisfy a side effect would have added several minutes to every push against this project's own explicit CI-wall-clock priority, and a hand-rolled schema-apply step would drift from the real migration. Instead `answer_general()` takes `record_provenance: bool = True` -- every real caller unchanged -- and `run_eval.py` passes `False`, which is also the *correct* behavior independent of CI: a golden question is synthetic eval traffic, and recording it would commingle synthetic runs into the same audit-quality table real citizen/worker answers use, while `run_eval.py` grades the returned answer directly and never reads a persisted row back. TDD, two tests written first and watched fail with the exact `TypeError` (`TestRecordProvenanceOptOut`, covering both the grounded and abstention paths, each asserting `provenance.record` is genuinely never called). `make lint` clean; `make test` green across the monorepo (Java 119, web 20, data-platform 29, `ai/` 119) before the first push. |

Pivoted to a hosted OpenRouter judge (a judge never serves user traffic, so this repo's self-hosting requirement for the *generation* model under test doesn't carry over to it): live-probed OpenRouter's own `openrouter/free` auto-router first and found it can silently route to a safety-classifier model that ignores the actual prompt, so pinned instead to a specific model verified live to handle structured JSON output cleanly (`nvidia/nemotron-3-ultra-550b-a55b:free`), after two candidates hit real 429s on their first call. Even so, 20 questions was too slow for a per-push gate, so `_CI_GATE_QUESTIONS` is a stratified 8-question subset (one or two per coverage area); the full ~20-question golden set stays committed, runnable via `--full`. **A real, live production bug found while measuring, not a test artifact**: two of the eight CI-gate questions crashed with `PromptTooLongError` -- every categorical-eligibility golden question retrieves 7 CFR 273.2(j)(1) or (j)(2)(E), real subsections with no further internal CFR structure to split on (`corpus/chunk.py`'s own documented exception to its `MAX_CHUNK_CHARS` cap, sized for the *embedding* model's window, not the generation model's), and a few such chunks retrieved together push the assembled prompt over `ollama_num_ctx`'s budget. This is not eval-only: `answer_general()` is the same code path a live user hits, so this would have crashed a real citizen's or worker's question about categorical eligibility too. Fixed with TDD (`test_a_prompt_too_long_for_the_model_abstains_instead_of_crashing`, written first, watched fail with the real uncaught exception): `qa/service.py`'s `_retrieve_and_answer` now catches `PromptTooLongError` and abstains, the same "can't reliably answer this" treatment already used for an ungrounded generation -- a retry is skipped deliberately, since the context-fit check is deterministic on the same prompt and would fail identically. **A second real, live failure found while re-measuring**: a clean local run (isolated, nothing else running concurrently) got through all 8 questions' retrieval and generation in ~7:30, then the very first judge call failed with a genuine OpenRouter upstream error, `{'message': 'Upstream error from Nvidia: Service temporarily overloaded', 'code': 502}` -- a transient free-tier capacity issue with nothing wrong in the request. Fixed with TDD (`test_judge_model.py`, 3 tests): `OpenRouterJudgeModel.generate` now retries up to 3 attempts with backoff on retryable codes (429/502/503), and still raises immediately on a non-retryable one. **A process lesson, not a code fix**: the first attempt at this same measurement, run *with* `ruff`/`mypy`/`pytest` running concurrently in this same session, took 49:36 before an Ollama `httpx.ReadTimeout` crashed it outright -- the identical measurement in isolation completed in 7-14 minutes. This host's Ollama+OpenSearch containers already sit at ~80% of its 7.65GiB baseline (`docker stats`, confirmed live) before any test load, consistent with this project's own extensive prior circuit-breaker history; not a CI-specific risk, since each CI job is its own isolated runner, but a real constraint on how this measurement had to be taken locally. **The real, human-reviewed `baseline.json`** (never auto-written, per the plan's own requirement): `faithfulness: 0.750`, `contextual_precision: 1.000`, `contextual_recall: 1.000`, from a clean run where `citation_grounded_rate` was `0.500` (4 of 8) -- 2 of those 4 ungrounded questions are the two categorical-eligibility ones above (deterministic every run), the other 2 reflect this small local model's ordinary citation variance already documented elsewhere in this log; `citation_grounded_rate` itself isn't gated (see `run_eval.py`'s `_METRIC_NAMES`), reported for visibility only. The `ai-eval` CI job's `timeout-minutes` corrected from a guessed 40 to a measured-and-margined 30, matching `e2e-ai`'s own budget for the same opensearch/ollama/corpus-index setup plus the real 13:41 measurement. README's architecture diagram checked -- no change: it depicts user-facing system components only, and Task 7 is CI tooling with no user-facing surface. |
| 2026-08-24 | Fix: Task 5's `analytics_copilot` tool-call args rejected a JSON-encoded-string array from Ollama -- `ai/src/canopica_ai/analytics_copilot/tools.py`, `ai/tests/test_analytics_copilot.py` | Found while investigating Task 6's own CI push (run `32775758436`, `End-to-end -- AI layer` job) -- a **real, reproducible production bug in already-shipped Task 5 code**, caught live in CI, not a test flake: `llama3.2:3b`'s tool call returned `group_by='["determination__is_expedited"]'` and `filters='["SNAP"]'` as JSON-encoded *strings* rather than native JSON arrays, even though the tool schema declares both as arrays -- `QueryMetricArgs.model_validate()` rejected both with `pydantic_core.ValidationError`, meaning any real worker's question that triggered a `group_by`/`filters` argument had a real chance of failing the same way, not just this test. TDD: wrote two failing tests first (`test_a_json_encoded_string_group_by_is_coerced_to_a_list`, `test_a_json_encoded_string_filters_is_coerced_to_a_list`), watched them fail with the exact CI error, then fixed with a `mode="before"` field validator that JSON-decodes a string only when it actually parses to a list -- anything else (a non-JSON string, a JSON object/scalar) still fails validation exactly as before, so this isn't a general "accept anything" escape hatch (`test_a_non_json_string_group_by_still_fails_validation` pins that). Re-ran the exact live-Ollama e2e test that failed in CI (`TestAskWithARealModel::test_a_real_question_resolves_to_the_correct_metric_and_number`) against the real model locally: passes (75.83s). The same CI run's second failure (`ml-commons task ... failed: Network is unreachable`, cascading into every OpenSearch-dependent test via a session fixture) traces to `ensure_reranker_model`'s prebuilt-model registration attempting an external download mid-CI -- unrelated to any code this session touched (Task 6 never touches `search_pipeline.py`/OpenSearch); treated as a transient runner network blip, not a code bug, and left for the next push to confirm rather than chased with a speculative fix. `ai/` now 93 non-e2e + 18 e2e (111 total, up from 108). **This fix's own push (run `32779851368`) then failed a *third* time, on a genuinely different error** -- checked against the real job log rather than assumed identical: no `ValidationError` this time (the fix held), but `MetricCompilationError`, because the model chose the wrong *metric* (`determinations`, a bare count) for "average processing time... broken out by whether expedited," instead of `processing_days_avg`. MetricFlow's own compiler correctly refused rather than silently running the wrong query -- the system did exactly what its "explains, never decides" backstop exists for. Root cause check: `ai/src/canopica_ai/analytics_copilot/tools.py`'s own tool-description example (`determination__is_expedited`) is valid syntax, just only for the *correct* metric (`sem_processing_timeliness`'s primary entity is `determination`) -- the model applied it to the wrong one. Genuine model-selection variance on a real, un-mocked 3B-model e2e test, not a regression from this fix or a code defect; re-ran via `gh run rerun 32779851368 --failed` and it landed green, all 11 jobs. Left as observed behavior rather than a Task 5 prompt-engineering fix, which is out of this session's scope. |
