## Decisions already made (don't relitigate)

Recorded here so a fresh session doesn't reopen settled questions. Full
reasoning lives in the design docs.

| Decision | Choice | Where |
|---|---|---|
| DMN runtime | Drools/KIE, not Camunda | Roadmap §3.3 |
| Reporting | TMDL model-as-code + Power BI Service + Metabase container | Roadmap §3.3 |
| Audit log | Hash-chained, CI-verified | Roadmap §3.6 |
| Phase 1 shape | Split 1a / 1b | Roadmap §5 |
| Unit of eligibility | `PROGRAM_REQUEST`, not `APPLICATION` | Roadmap §3.4.1 |
| Determinations | Append-only; a change produces a new one | Roadmap §3.4.1 |
| Policy parameters | Effective-dated and immutable once published | Roadmap §3.5 |
| AI scope | All nine components stay in committed scope | Roadmap §5 |
| Cloud target | Azure Government by design; commercial Azure for any live demo | Roadmap §3.7 |
| Repo visibility | Stays private during active development, deliberately left without branch rulesets/protection — GitHub only allows those on a private repo with GitHub Pro (personal-account Free-plan limit, confirmed against the API). Going public was offered and declined 2026-08-22; confirmed 2026-08-23 the plan is to flip to public once development is complete (not before), at which point branch protection becomes free again and the README's CI badge — added 2026-08-23, harmless while private, invisible to a signed-out viewer until the flip — starts rendering for anyone. | — |
| Java build tool | Maven (not Gradle) — dominant in the enterprise/government Java shops this repo targets; committed `./mvnw` wrapper is what CI runs | Phase 1a plan, "Versions pinned" |
| Python version | 3.12 via `uv`, not the system 3.14 — dbt-core support lags new releases | Phase 1a plan, "Versions pinned" |
| Bronze storage, Phase 1a | Local filesystem Delta tables; MinIO/S3 is a `storage_options` swap in Phase 1b | Phase 1a plan, Task 10 |
| Async messaging | **pgmq** (Postgres extension), not RabbitMQ/Kafka — document-intake jobs, correspondence dispatch, fraud-triage triggers. Portfolio-documented substitute, not an oversight. | Roadmap §3.3; tech-stack doc's Messaging tier + §4.11 |
| Ingestion pattern | Batch extract (Python job) for Phase 1a; CDC/Debezium documented as the production equivalent, not built — same treatment as pgmq, and for the same reason (needs a broker). | Roadmap §3.3; tech-stack doc's Data tier + §4.12 |
| Row-level auth model | Explicit `CASE_ASSIGNMENT` per household (not implicit by county); auto-claimed on first worker touch; `SUPERVISOR` can view any case, logged (not blocked) via a `CASE_VIEWED` payload flag; sensitive-case flag is flag-and-log, not sealing | Phase 1b design doc §2.1; tech-stack doc's Authorization row |
| Mock external verification shape | Synchronous REST, deterministic canned responses keyed by input hash, every request/response an audit-chain event, raw response gated by an active `CASE_ASSIGNMENT` | Phase 1b design doc §2.2; tech-stack doc's External verification row |
| PII tokenization | `pii_token` vault table (`pgcrypto`-encrypted), narrow-RLS'd; detokenization is a separate audited call, not a normal column read | Phase 1b design doc §2.3; tech-stack doc's Data tier + §4.15 |
| `mart_fairness_audit` timing | Deferred from Phase 1b to Phase 4, built alongside fraud-triage so it has both comparison axes from day one | Phase 1b design doc §2.4 |
| Observability stack | Jaeger (traces) + Prometheus/Grafana (metrics), self-hosted single-host — not a heavier collector/Tempo/Loki stack | Phase 1b design doc §2.5; tech-stack doc's Observability row |
| Reference Terraform compute | Azure Container Apps, not AKS (the plan's own explicit either/or) — simpler to reference without a Kubernetes manifest layer, consistent with this project's containers-not-clusters posture elsewhere; resource list matches the plan's "Resources modeled" exactly (Keycloak/Metabase/Jaeger-Prometheus-Grafana deliberately absent) | Phase 1b plan Task 10; `infra/azure/README.md`; STATUS.md's Task 10 verification row |
| Policy corpus | Real public 7 CFR Part 273 text (eCFR/GovInfo), scoped to just the sections this project's own rules engine implements, chunked at CFR subsection level | Phase 2 design doc §2.1 |
| Retrieval pipeline | Hybrid (BM25 + k-NN) via Reciprocal Rank Fusion, then a cross-encoder reranking pass — not hybrid search alone | Phase 2 design doc §2.1; tech-stack doc's Embeddings + retrieval row |
| RAG answer provenance | Every answer records corpus/embedding-model/prompt/generation-config versions plus the retrieved chunk IDs — same reproducibility discipline as determinations | Phase 2 design doc §2.1/§2.2 |
| Rule-authoring copilot scope | Proposes new `policy_parameter_set` values, not DMN decision-table restructuring — a real narrowing from the roadmap's own "DMN diff" wording | Phase 2 design doc §2.3; roadmap doc §3.3 |
| Parameter-set supersession | Publishing a superseding set closes the outgoing one by setting `effective_to` `null` → date, the one UPDATE V3's trigger permits (one-way, and only when every other column is bit-identical). Keeps ranges non-overlapping so `findEffectiveOn` still errors on ambiguity rather than silently picking; provably outside §3.5's reproducibility guarantee, since re-derivation resolves by parameter-set *id* and reads neither date column. Forced by Phase 2 Task 3 — the first path that publishes a set at runtime. | `docs/design/2026-08-23-policy-parameter-supersession.md`; roadmap doc §3.3 |
| Analytics Copilot mechanism | MetricFlow exposed over MCP as callable tools; authorization resolved at tool-exposure time, before query compilation | Phase 2 design doc §2.4; roadmap doc §3.3 |
| Analytics Copilot execution engine & backstop | MetricFlow queries run against DuckDB (where dbt actually builds gold, not Postgres); backstop is `read_only=True` + `enable_external_access=false` + `lock_configuration=true` + file protection, verified by local probe and cross-checked against DuckDB's own security docs — not a Postgres role. `canopica_analytics_ro` still exists, relabeled to guard the separate Postgres serving layer (Metabase/Power BI), not the copilot. Approved 2026-08-24. | `2026-08-24-analytics-semantic-layer-execution-and-authorization.md`; roadmap doc §3.3; tech-stack doc's AI tier row + §4.17 |
| Eval-gate method | RAGAS metrics (faithfulness, context precision/recall) gated via DeepEval with baseline-relative thresholds, plus a deterministic citation-existence pre-check | Phase 2 design doc §2.6; roadmap doc §3.3 |
| Eval-gate judge model | Hosted OpenRouter, **paid** — `deepseek/deepseek-chat`, not local Ollama and no longer the free `nvidia/nemotron-3-ultra-550b-a55b:free` this row originally named. Revised 2026-08-26 after that free-tier model produced two same-week genuine upstream 502s that exhausted a widened retry window; DeepSeek picked live against three other paid candidates on real pricing (DeepSeek $0.2574/$1.029, GPT-5 Mini $0.25/$2.00, Gemini 2.5 Flash $0.30/$2.50, Claude Haiku 4.5 $1/$5 per MTok) and confirmed clean on a real 12-question run (`faithfulness 1.000`, no retries). A judge never serves user traffic, so the "self-hosted, $0" requirement for the *generation* model under test (unchanged) doesn't carry over to it either way. | Phase 2 design doc §2.6 addendum; `ai/src/canopica_ai/policy_intelligence/eval/judge_model.py`, `config.py`, `run_eval.py` module docstrings |
| Public demo inference | OpenRouter free-tier models first, falling back to a cheap paid OpenRouter model under a $5/mo hard cap during real traffic, only failing closed once both are exhausted. **Which paid model, staged in two steps (decided 2026-08-26):** `deepseek/deepseek-chat` for the entire remaining dev period — cheapest option (~4x under Claude Haiku 4.5 on this call shape) and already proven live as the eval judge above — then repointed to `anthropic/claude-haiku-4.5` in the same commit as the public-repo flip, for a spec-clean `gen_ai.provider.name` value and to avoid a China-based model provider being the one answering a benefits-eligibility-flavored public demo. Both models are reachable through the same `OpenRouterTieredClient`/OpenRouter account — this is a one-line config value, not a second provider integration, so the swap costs nothing beyond remembering to do it. Superseded a same-day earlier direction to add Claude as a genuinely separate provider (`2026-08-26-anthropic-tiered-inference-provider.md`'s Option C) once DeepSeek's live-proven reliability made a second full provider integration unnecessary for solving the original problem (OpenRouter free-tier flakiness). | Phase 2 design doc §2.7; roadmap doc §3.3; tech-stack doc's Public-demo inference row + §4.16; `docs/design/2026-08-26-anthropic-tiered-inference-provider.md` (superseded, kept as-is per this project's own record-not-rewrite convention) |
| Public demo hosting | Fly.io — **not free** (corrected 2026-08-26); real cost **now pinned, not estimated**: `infra/fly/fly.toml` (written 2026-08-26) configures `shared-cpu-2x`/4GB — the smallest Fly tier that actually fits OpenSearch's own 2048m JVM heap plus its off-heap usage, Ollama's daemon, and the Python app (shared-cpu-1x's 2GB ceiling doesn't). Live-priced against `fly.io/docs/about/pricing/` (Amsterdam rate, 2026-08-26): ~$22.22/mo compute (shared-cpu-2x/4GB) + $0.15/GB/mo for a 1GB volume holding only the OpenRouter spend-cap counter file (not OpenSearch's data — that corpus is baked into the image at build time, not volume-mounted, so a volume can't accidentally shadow it) ≈ **~$22/mo all-in**, superseding both the original "~$5/mo" figure (assumed a machine a quarter this size) and the interim "$15-20/mo, not yet pinned" estimate two rows up in the verification log. Real per-region pricing may vary slightly from the Amsterdam rate quoted; exact billed amount still confirms only at the actual Step 5 deploy. Fly.io removed free-tier signups in October 2024 (pay-as-you-go only, card required); the design doc's and plan's original "free/cheap" framing was stale from the day it was written. Render (the other option §2.7 named) is ruled out on a hard constraint, not cost — its free web services have no persistent disk. Vercel was raised and ruled out on an even harder constraint: its serverless functions support no persistent process and no arbitrary TCP ports, so an always-resident, disk-backed OpenSearch process cannot run there on any plan. Fly.io stays the pick because it's still the cheapest option that actually satisfies the requirement, not because it's cheap in absolute terms. | Phase 2 design doc §2.7 (corrected); implementation plan's hosting-pick bullet + Task 9 Step 5 (corrected); `infra/fly/fly.toml` |
| Phase 5 domain expansion scope | **Neither TANF nor Medicaid gets built — narrowed twice the same day.** First narrowed to TANF-only (Medicaid stated as extension point), then narrowed again once the user posed the sharper question directly: would a prospective employer evaluating senior-engineer/architect-level work care how many benefits programs a demo repo covers, versus the engineering depth underneath one of them? TANF would have mostly re-exercised the same DMN/audit/warehouse architecture SNAP already proves — repeated engineering effort, not new signal. **Both** TANF and Medicaid are now recorded as deliberately-not-built extension points, stated rather than silently absent, per this project's own "considered, deliberately deferred" convention — backed by a real, inspectable detail rather than an unsupported claim: `determination` already carries a program-agnostic `program_code` field (visible in the Analytics Copilot's own semantic layer), so the generalization claim rests on something in the code, not just prose. The actual "scaled this to multiple programs" story is real professional experience to state in an interview, not portfolio-repo work to redo. | Roadmap doc's Phase 5 section (renamed "Cloud realization," corrected); CLAUDE.md, README.md, this file's Phase table (all corrected same day) |
| AI capability architecture | Every AI feature is a fixed, bounded pipeline (retrieve→generate, or NL→tool-call→execute) — never an open-ended autonomous agent | Phase 2 design doc §2.10; roadmap doc §3.3 |
| Phase 3 design | Case Intake & Communication AI. New infra: MinIO (object storage, already decided at the tier level in the tradeoffs doc but never stood up), `pgmq` (queue mechanism decided in Phase 1, first real consumer here), and a new `worker/` deployable (Python, first async background process in this codebase) polling two separate pgmq queues (`document_intake`, `correspondence_dispatch`). Document Intake: LLM-native structured extraction (Pydantic-validated, not OCR-then-parse), confidence-gated review queue with **no confidence-based auto-apply bypass ever** — a deliberate divergence from the 2026 industry norm (commonly ~1-5% manual review), applying the governing principle literally to case-record writes. Correspondence: template-filling (not free-form generation-then-validation) — the LLM fills named slots against the determination trace/audit trail, every dollar amount/date substituted programmatically, a deterministic pre-check (mirrors Phase 2's citation-existence check) before human review, no auto-send path. `notice` is one table with a status lifecycle (`DRAFT`/`APPROVED`/`REJECTED`/`SENT`), not split into an advisory/binding pair the way `ai.policy_qa_answer` sits apart from `eligibility_determination` — closer in shape to `policy_parameter_proposal`. Translation split into UI i18n (pure tooling, `react-i18next`) versus correspondence translation (LLM, routed through the same validation/review gate as the English draft). Checked against 2026 practice before finalizing: LLM-native extraction over OCR-then-parse, grounded/template-based generation over free drafting, and a real finding not previously stated — `pgmq.send()` inside the same transaction as its triggering write gets transactional-outbox reliability structurally, for free, which a dedicated broker would need explicit outbox-table-plus-relay machinery to match. | `2026-08-27-phase-3-case-intake-communication-ai-design.md`; roadmap doc §3.3 (`worker/` in repo layout, pgmq row), tradeoffs doc (Document intake/Correspondence rows) |
| Phase 4 design | Compliance & Integrity AI. Not greenfield: 4 of 5 needed gold marts already exist from Phase 1b (`mart_processing_timeliness`, `mart_determination_outcomes`, `mart_worker_caseload`, `mart_access_review`); `mart_payment_accuracy` exists as an explicit placeholder; only `mart_fairness_audit` is genuinely new. Fraud Risk Triage: unsupervised anomaly scoring (`scikit-learn` `IsolationForest` over engineered features — income volatility, verification-discrepancy rate, household-change frequency, benefit-amount percentile within cohort), not a supervised classifier — no real fraud-labeled data exists in this synthetic-only project. Triggered by a third pgmq queue (`fraud_scoring`) enqueued in the same transaction as a determination commit, same shape as Phase 3's correspondence trigger. No auto-adjudication: a score above threshold writes a `fraud_risk_score` row and surfaces the case to a review queue, nothing about the determination itself changes. Explicitly excludes race/ethnicity/national-origin proxies (zip code, surname, language); `mart_fairness_audit` extends the *same* disparate-impact-ratio CI gate and Power BI page the rules engine already has to this second model, one table/one gate for both. QC/Payment Error Rate Assistant: re-runs the original DMN determination as-of its own stored parameter-set version (reuses the existing §3.5 reproducibility guarantee, no new Java infrastructure) against a *sampled*, not exhaustive, batch — modeled on the real federal SNAP QC process, which samples too; AI's role is a plain-language discrepancy summary composed from the diff and both determinations' trace records, never the LLM's own arithmetic. SLA/Compliance Monitor: the "at-risk" queue for still-pending cases is a live **operational** API query (`program_request` aged against the 7/30-day standard), not a dbt mart, because it needs same-day currency; AI prioritizes and drafts a one-line stall reason grounded in real audit/verification fields. SOP Copilot: retrieve→generate, identical shape to Policy Q&A, against a small *authored* (not sourced — no public SOP corpus exists for this project) markdown corpus in a separate OpenSearch index, same abstain-on-weak-match discipline. SOP Mining: extends the existing Analytics Copilot (MetricFlow-over-MCP) mechanism with new metrics, no new architecture, and has no write path — an SOP change is a human/process action outside the app. Data-quality anomaly detection: adopts Elementary (dbt-native, self-hosted, $0) rather than a hand-rolled detector — already named in the tradeoffs doc as this project's stated, unbuilt production-equivalent gap; AI drafts a root-cause summary on a dbt-test or Elementary-anomaly failure, surfaced via a new `data_quality_incident` table and a small reporting page (no chat-ops/Slack integration — not carried elsewhere in this stack). Roles: no new Keycloak role — `SUPERVISOR` (already "can view any case") covers both the fraud-review queue and QC review; the roadmap's "investigator"/"QC reviewer" language names functions, not new personas. Checked against 2026 practice before finalizing (findings noted inline in the design doc rather than folded in silently): unsupervised anomaly-first scoring as the standard shape for unlabeled fraud triage, and Elementary's adoption path. | `2026-08-29-phase-4-compliance-integrity-ai-design.md`; roadmap doc §3.3 (fairness-gate and no-proxy-features rows already cover both models); tradeoffs doc's AI tier (Fraud risk triage row), Data tier (Data quality observability row), §4.20, §5, §6.5 |
| UI modernization design system | "Public Ledger" — civic-editorial visual language (warm paper base, Canopica Verdigris `#167C6B` for governed/affirmed states, DM Serif Display + DM Sans, case-file-desk layout with a chain-of-custody spine and decision bar) on a Tailwind + shadcn/ui foundation. Scoped to the 5 pages that exist today; Phase 3/4 screens get it once those phases are real, not before. Sourced from a Manus design exploration kept in a separate scratch repo (reference only — not merged, not a dependency) and re-implemented natively. Decided 2026-08-27. **Addendum, same day, mid-execution (Tasks 1–2 committed, Task 3 implemented, uncommitted):** a second exploration, five Google Stitch–generated screens, was reviewed and disposed of screen-by-screen — Stitch's own generated token system (black primary, Inter, slate/navy) was **not** adopted, same reasoning as the Manus boundary above (reference, not merge); Public Ledger's already-approved, contrast-verified tokens are unchanged. Adopted: an `AiAdvisoryBadge` built on the already-defined-but-unused `--info` token, marking AI-generated content as advisory-only (closes a real gap against this doc's own §3 principle and CLAUDE.md's governing principle — nothing in the app visually made that distinction before); a `CalculationMatrix` table view of `TraceResponse.decisionResults` alongside `CustodySpine`; `TopUtilityBar`'s breadcrumb/case-id slot (already spec'd in §6, not yet built). Two genuinely new, non-restyle tasks added: Task 7 (an audit-trail read endpoint — verified live that none exists today, `AuditService` only exposes `append` — plus a richer `CaseDetailPage` display) and Task 8 (a Caseworker Dashboard page, worker-caseload-scoped real case counts only). Declined: Stitch's manual if/then rule-builder (doesn't match `RuleAuthoringPage`'s actual paste-excerpt→AI-drafts→human-reviews flow) and its Data Governance & Lineage screen (Phase 2/3+ territory with no current `ui/` page — mocking it now repeats the exact mistake §4's own Manus exclusion already ruled out). Also explicitly declined within Task 8: the mockup's SLA Compliance %, QC Accuracy %, and Integration Health stats — no backing definition exists anywhere in Canopica for any of the three, and shipping a fabricated number next to real determinism everywhere else in this app would undercut this project's whole premise. **Second addendum, same day, direction change:** after all 6 original pages were migrated to Public Ledger and reviewed live, the user's assessment was that it read as flat and under-polished — "amateur," not "civic-editorial." Given a direct choice between fixing execution within Public Ledger or changing direction, the user chose to change direction, anchored on the Stitch mockups (reviewed above) as the concrete quality bar: Stitch's own token system — previously declined — is now adopted, read directly from its rendered HTML rather than prose (`03-case-review-audit-trail.html`'s literal hex overrides, not its Material-3-token-named Tailwind config, which resolves to a different, warmer `#fcf8fa`). Background `#F8FAFC`, elevated white cards with real shadow, near-black text, Inter throughout (no serif display face), a tight `0.125rem`/`0.25rem`/`0.5rem` radius scale (corrected during implementation: the foundation commit initially left the old Public Ledger `--radius: 0.625rem` calc-derived scale in place under the new palette — caught by cross-checking the design doc's own stated values against `index.css` before committing, fixed to literal values matching Stitch's real `borderRadius` config), and a bottom-anchored `NavRail` account-controls section (role + sign out) closing a real gap the user flagged directly ("we cant leave it hanging after the tabs") — modeled on Stitch's own `mt-auto border-t` sidebar-footer pattern but with real functionality, not Stitch's own non-functional Settings/Help placeholder links. Public Ledger is superseded on visual language only, not technique — Tailwind/shadcn foundation, page-by-page migration discipline, the accessibility gate, and the component set (`CalculationMatrix`/`CustodySpine`/`DecisionBar`/`PageChrome`) all carry forward re-skinned, not rebuilt. | `2026-08-27-ui-modernization-public-ledger.md` §11; `2026-08-27-ui-modernization-implementation-plan.md` (Tasks 3/5/6 amended, Tasks 7–8 added); `2026-08-27-ui-modernization-stitch-direction.md`; roadmap doc §3.3 |

| `e2e-ai`'s `TestAnswerDenial` (Policy Q&A explanation) and `TestAskWithARealModel` (Analytics Copilot) may share one root cause: this VM's RAM is undersized for the job's own concurrent stack | No -- both pre-existing, candidate fix now CI-confirmed for `e2e-ai` as a whole | Run `32967528722`, `e2e-ai`: `TestAnswerDenial` failed on a plain `httpx.ReadTimeout` submitting an application -- not the `worker_email_key` collision from the previous run, which is confirmed gone. Measured at the moment of failure: **273Mi RAM available of 7.8Gi, plus 1.7Gi of the 4Gi swap already in use** -- `docker stats` at that instant shows `opensearch` (2.276GiB) and `ollama` (2.914GiB) alone resident at 5.19GiB for the job's entire lifetime, before `api`/`keycloak`/`postgres`/`jaeger` get anything. In the same run, `TestAskWithARealModel` failed on `MetricCompilationError` **despite already carrying two independent layers of retry** from the 2026-08-25 fix (`4a8d48c`), together live-measured at ~90% per-attempt success -- two independent draws failing back to back is roughly a 1%-if-independent event, a real reason to suspect memory pressure degraded generation quality rather than ordinary sampling variance. **Candidate fix applied 2026-08-26**: VM resized `Standard_D2s_v7` -> `Standard_D4s_v7` (2 vCPU/8GB -> 4 vCPU/16GB) via `az vm resize` (see that date's verification-log row for why not `terraform apply`), +$0.018/run. **Confirmed 2026-08-27**: CI run `33037187113`'s `e2e-ai` job went fully green (7m42s) -- the first real run of this job since the resize. Note this doesn't touch `ai-eval`'s own circuit-breaker failure in the same run, which is a different, host-RAM-independent cause (OpenSearch's heap is hardcoded to a fixed size regardless of host RAM) -- see the top verification-log row. |

| `cloud-init.yaml`'s hook scripts use `$$(...)` for command substitution, but `templatefile()` only collapses `$${` -> `${`, not a bare `$$(` | **Yes, before the next real `terraform apply`/VM recreate** -- a fresh deploy from the committed source would render broken bash | Found 2026-08-26 while adding the checkout-guard line to `job-started-hook.sh` (see that date's verification-log row). Confirmed empirically with `terraform console` against an isolated test file: `$${leftovers}` -> `${leftovers}` (correct), but `$$(docker ps -aq)` -> unchanged `$$(docker ps -aq)`, and `bash -n` confirms `$$(` is a syntax error. Every `$$(...)` in both `job-started-hook.sh` and `job-completed-hook.sh` has this latent bug. It's invisible today only because the live VM was hand-patched out-of-band (the same `custom_data`-drift pattern the 2026-08-26 CI-caching row already flagged) with the correct single-`$` form, so the working script and the committed source have quietly diverged. Fix (not yet done): change every `$$(` to `$(` in the template source, since command substitution has no `${` in it and never needed escaping in the first place; re-verify with the same `terraform console` isolated-file technique before trusting it. |

| `gh run rerun --failed` doesn't restart `start-runner`, but `stop-runner` already deallocated the VM at the end of the original run | No — known now, workaround exists | Hit live 2026-08-26: after `gh run rerun --failed` on `e2e-ai`, the queued job sat unpicked for 6+ minutes because `rerun --failed` only re-executes previously-failed jobs, never an already-succeeded one like `start-runner` — and `stop-runner` had already deallocated `canopica-ci-runner-vm` at the end of the original run, so there was no runner left to claim it. Fixed by manually running `az vm start --resource-group canopica-ci-runner-rg --name canopica-ci-runner-vm`, after which the queued job started immediately. Workaround, not yet a permanent fix: any future `gh run rerun --failed` on a run whose `stop-runner` job already completed needs this same manual VM start first. A real fix would need `start-runner` to also run (or be reordered as) a no-op-safe check that happens on every rerun regardless of job history, which hasn't been designed. |

| `KeycloakWorkerSyncFilter` finds an existing `worker` row by `keycloak_subject` only, not `email` — a Postgres volume that outlives a Keycloak container restart (dev-mode `start-dev --import-realm` reissues a fresh subject UUID per seeded user on every boot) hits `worker_email_key`'s unique constraint on that worker's next login | No — local dev/test hygiene, not a production correctness bug (a real Keycloak deployment doesn't reissue subject ids on restart) | Hit live during Phase 1b Task 9's verification; worked around with `make down -v` before that task's own verification, not patched (Task 1's domain, not Task 9's). **Resurfaced 2026-08-26 as a real CI failure**, which is worth recording because it changes the risk assessment: on the long-lived self-hosted runner the operational Postgres volume began outliving the Keycloak container, so run `32935635062`'s `e2e-ai` hit `worker_email_key` on two tests. The runner's pre-job hook now prunes named volumes (`docker volume prune -af`, see that date's verification-log row), which removes the *trigger* on CI but leaves this filter bug exactly as it was -- any environment where a Postgres volume outlives a Keycloak restart still hits it. |
| The two `e2e` jobs each rebuild `api` from scratch, uncached -- the largest remaining CI cost | No -- CI is green and usable; this is throughput, not correctness | Next lever after the 2026-08-23 CI work (see the verification-log rows for what was already done). Both `e2e-data-platform` and `e2e-ai` run `docker compose up -d --build`, and `api/Dockerfile`'s builder stage runs `./mvnw -B -q -pl api -am package -DskipTests` with no Docker layer cache and no Maven cache, so every run re-resolves and re-downloads the full dependency tree twice, once per job. The `java` job builds the same artifact a third time, but that one is cached (`setup-java` with `cache: maven`, ~105s). Measured: the Compose bring-up step is 256-392s, and the swing between two otherwise-identical runs is itself mostly Maven download variance -- it is now both the biggest single step and the noisiest. Candidate approaches, none tried yet: `docker/build-push-action` with `cache-from/cache-to: type=gha`; or Buildx bake via `COMPOSE_BAKE=true` with the same GHA cache backend; or mounting a shared Maven cache into the builder stage via BuildKit `--mount=type=cache`. Worth roughly 100-200s per run and, unlike most of the earlier work, it compounds -- it also speeds up `ui` and the two Airflow images in the data-platform job. |
| An infrastructure failure is currently indistinguishable from a genuine abstention | **Yes, for trust -- no, for shipping Phase 2.** Not a regression and not blocking Task 3, but it undermines the one guarantee the abstention path exists to give | Found 2026-08-23 while diagnosing a real `e2e` failure (`dfe2da7`, and again locally). When Ollama's `llama-server` is OOM-killed mid-run, the next generation comes back uncited; `service.py`'s grounding retry fires, also comes back uncited, and the system abstains -- writing an `ai.policy_qa_answer` row that says "insufficient information in the policy corpus to answer this". That row asserts something false *about the corpus* when the actual cause was the inference process dying. Design doc §2.2's abstention is an epistemic claim ("the corpus can't support an answer"), so silently overloading it with "the infrastructure broke" is a real correctness bug in a trust-critical path, and it also makes the failure undiagnosable after the fact. A hard 5xx is already handled correctly (`raise_for_status()` surfaces it); the gap is a *successful* response returning degraded output after a restart. Two candidate fixes, neither started: record an explicit abstention reason (retrieval-score vs ungrounded-generation) on the provenance row, which needs a migration and makes spurious abstentions visible; and/or treat an empty or implausibly short generation as an error rather than as evidence about the corpus. Deliberately not bundled into the generation-tuning commit that found it -- CLAUDE.md's scoped-changes convention -- and worth doing before Phase 2's eval gate (Task 8) starts scoring abstention rates, since a spurious abstention would otherwise silently corrupt that metric. |
